https://math.stanford.edu/~vakil/greatwriting.html, Ravi Vakil on Mathematical Writing. Advice, reflections, and guidance on writing clearly and effectively in mathematics.

https://math.uchicago.edu/~emerton/gl2.pdf, Adelic and Classical Modular Forms for GL(2). An exposition of the translation between classical modular forms and automorphic forms on the adelic group $\mathrm{GL}_2(\mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}})$. Provides a geometric interpretation of modular forms as sections of line bundles over modular curves, along with minimal ad hoc constructions of Eisenstein series. Details the Lie algebra $\mathfrak{gl}_2(\mathbb{R})$ with basis elements $Z, H, Y_+, Y_-$, where the center $\mathcal{Z}(\mathfrak{gl}_2)$ of the universal enveloping algebra is isomorphic to a polynomial ring in two generators. Explores the space of bases $B$ of $\mathbb{C}$ as a real vector space, identifying it as an open subset of $\mathbb{C}^2$ with commuting left and right actions by $\mathrm{GL}_2(\mathbb{R})$ and $\mathrm{GL}_1(\mathbb{C})$. The right action of $\mathrm{GL}_2(\mathbb{R})$ is complex analytic and gives an identification with the upper half-plane $\mathcal{H}^{\pm} \times \mathbb{C}^\times$ via $(\tau, z) \mapsto \left(\frac{a\tau+c}{b\tau+d}, (b\tau+d)z\right)$. Examines the complexified Lie algebra tangent space decomposition into holomorphic and anti-holomorphic subspaces, the subspace $V_i \subset \mathfrak{gl}_2$, and the bijection $B/\mathrm{GL}_2(\mathbb{Z}) \cong \mathcal{L}$ with the moduli of discrete rank-2 lattices. Covers moderate growth conditions, smooth adelic automorphic forms, and representations invariant under right translation by $\mathrm{GL}_2(\mathbb{A}_f)$.

https://publications.ias.edu/sites/default/files/5_FormesModulaires.pdf, Deligne on Modular Forms. Foundational notes by Pierre Deligne on the algebraic and geometric theory of modular forms, referenced in Emerton's notes.

https://www.intlpress.com/site/pub/files/_fulltext/journals/cdm/1997/1997/0001/CDM-1997-1997-0001-a001.pdf, Algebraic Geometry and Arithmetic Duality. Connects the eigenvalues of the Frobenius action on étale cohomology $H_{\text{ét}}^*(X, \mathbb{Q}_\ell)$ to the zeros and poles of zeta functions, completing the arithmetic dictionary via the adèle class space.

https://indico.math.cnrs.fr/event/10843/contributions/12868/, Class Field Theory and Noncommutative Geometry. An exposition by Alain Connes on class field theory through the lens of the adèle class space $X_{\mathbb{Q}} = \mathbb{Q}^\times \backslash \mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}$. Abelian extensions of a number field $K$ are encoded internally by its idele class group $C_K = \mathbb{A}_K^\times / K^\times$, acting as a Galois group of the maximal abelian extension via Artin reciprocity. Galois actions on roots are realized through group actions on spaces. The quotient space $X_K = K^\times \backslash \mathbb{A}_K$ is ergodic with respect to the Haar measure, serving as a prototype noncommutative space (leaf space of a foliation). Translates local-to-global class field theory via the inclusion of local isotropy groups $K_v^\times \hookrightarrow C_K$. Connects the Riemann–Weil explicit formulas $\sum_v \int f(u)/|1-u|\,d^*u$ to a trace formula for the scaling operator on the adèle class space. Introduces the scaling site $X_{\mathbb{Q}} = \mathbb{Q}^\times \backslash \mathbb{A}_{\mathbb{Q}} / \widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^\times$, modeled as a noncommutative space of rank-1 subgroups of $\mathbb{R}$, where periodic orbits of length $\log p$ correspond to prime numbers $p$, realizing the Frobenius endomorphism as a mapping torus. Relates this construction to Grothendieck's étale fundamental group $\pi_1^{\text{ét}}$ and the 3D topology of $\operatorname{Spec} \mathbb{Z}$.

https://math.berkeley.edu/~scanlon/papers/O-min-midrasha.pdf, Overview of O-Minimality. Scanlon's survey on o-minimal structures, tame topology, and their applications in model theory and arithmetic geometry.

https://math.stackexchange.com/questions/1897078/maximal-abelian-extension-unramified-outside-a-set-of-primes, Maximal Abelian Extensions Unramified Outside S. Discussion and computation of the maximal abelian extension $K_S^{\text{ab}}$ of a number field $K$ unramified outside a finite set of places $S$. For a singleton prime $S = \{p\}$, $K_S^{\text{ab}}$ is given by the direct limit of ray class fields modulo $\mathfrak{p}^n \mathcal{O}_K$. Explains ramification at infinite places and the Galois group $G_S(K)^{\text{ab}} = G_S(K) / \overline{[G_S(K), G_S(K)]}$. Details the connection to class field theory, Poitou–Tate duality, and the Kronecker–Weber theorem for $K = \mathbb{Q}$.

https://kskedlaya.org/18.786/ps10.pdf, Direct Proof of the Kronecker-Weber Theorem. Lemmermeyer's direct proof showing that every finite abelian extension of $\mathbb{Q}$ is contained in a cyclotomic field $\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_m)$.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1211.3256, Ergodicity of Global Fields on Adeles. Rigorous proof of the ergodicity of the action of $K^\times$ on the adèle ring $\mathbb{A}_K$ for global fields in both the number field and function field settings.

https://www.math.uchicago.edu/~emerton/pdffiles/reciprocity.pdf, Perspectives on Reciprocity Laws. Emerton's survey on reciprocity from Gauss's quadratic reciprocity to Langlands functoriality. Compares Dirichlet's analytic class number formula for $L(1, \chi)$ (automorphic side) with Dedekind's class number formula (motivic side) and discusses the Sato–Tate conjecture.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2210.01404, Categorical p-Adic Langlands Correspondence. Comprehensive 252-page treatise by Emerton and Helm formulating the categorical $p$-adic Langlands program in both Banach and rigid analytic settings.

https://math.stanford.edu/~conrad/papers/luminysga3.pdf, Reductive Group Schemes after SGA3. Brian Conrad's foundational notes on the theory and classification of reductive group schemes over general bases.

https://math.uchicago.edu/~emerton/research.html, Research Remarks by Matthew Emerton. Collection of notes, papers, and essays by Matthew Emerton on automorphic forms, Galois representations, and the Langlands program.

https://mathoverflow.net/questions/37021/is-fourier-analysis-a-special-case-of-representation-theory-or-an-analogue/37189#37189, Fourier Analysis vs Representation Theory. Discussion on the relationship between harmonic analysis and group representations. Highlights the Peter–Weyl theorem for compact groups $G$, decomposing $L^2(G) \cong \widehat{\bigoplus}_{\pi} V_\pi \otimes V_\pi^*$, and the Plancherel decomposition for non-compact type I groups via Harish-Chandra's work.

https://math.stackexchange.com/questions/58515/what-is-fourier-analysis-on-groups-and-does-it-have-applications-to-physics/58591#58591, Harmonic Analysis and Physics. Survey of Plancherel decompositions on reductive Lie groups and Bargmann's classification of irreducible unitary representations of $\mathrm{SL}_2(\mathbb{R})$ with physical applications.

https://mathoverflow.net/questions/14849/what-would-a-moral-proof-of-the-weil-conjectures-require/14852#14852, Conceptual Meaning of the Weil Conjectures. Discussion on the philosophical and geometric requirements for proving the Weil conjectures via Weil cohomology theories, standard conjectures, and motives.

https://mathoverflow.net/questions/10201/a-problem-of-shimura-and-its-relation-to-class-field-theory/10217#10217, Shimura's Problem and Class Field Theory. Explores the connection between polynomial splitting fields, ray class groups, and complex multiplication. Example: splitting of polynomials generating abelian extensions inside cyclotomic fields like $\mathbb{Q}(\zeta_7)$.

https://mathoverflow.net/questions/62155/how-to-find-the-varieties-whose-cohomology-realizes-certain-representations/62211#62211, Geometric Realization of Galois Representations. Realizing 2-dimensional Galois representations attached to modular forms inside the étale cohomology $H^1_{\text{ét}}(X_0(N)_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}}, \mathbb{Q}_\ell)$ of modular curves, leading to Shimura varieties.

https://www.math.stonybrook.edu/~mmovshev/MAT570Spring2008/BOOKS/atiyahbott_moment.pdf, The Moment Map and Equivariant Cohomology. Atiyah and Bott's foundational paper on the de Rham localization theorem in equivariant cohomology $H_G^*(M)$. Links Hamiltonian group actions, moment maps $\mu: M \to \mathfrak{g}^*$, symplectic reduction, and the Duistermaat–Heckman exact stationary phase formula. Connects equivariant localization to moduli spaces of bundles on Riemann surfaces and Yang–Mills theory.

https://math.uchicago.edu/~may/REU2019/REUPapers/Lee%2CJaeHee.pdf, Atiyah-Bott Localization in Geometry. Jae Hee Lee's expository REU paper detailing the Atiyah–Bott localization theorem, equivariant characteristic classes, and topological invariants of quotient manifolds.

http://bogomolov-lab.ru/G-sem/AJM-3-1-001-016.pdf, Moment Maps and Diffeomorphisms in Gauge Theory. Donaldson's paper interpreting the curvature of a connection on a surface principal bundle as a moment map for the gauge group action, applied to Yang–Mills equations.

https://math.uchicago.edu/~shmuel/AAT-readings/Combinatorial%20Geometry%2C%20Concentration%2C%20Real%20Algebraic%20Geometry/ball.pdf, Convex Body Geometry by Keith Ball. Comprehensive introduction to convex geometry, volume computation of Euclidean balls, the Hahn–Banach separation theorem, and extremal properties of polytopes and cubes.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2307.15106, Non-Perturbative BV Theory via Derived Geometry. Formulating non-perturbative Batalin–Vilkovisky (BV) quantization within derived differential geometry and shifted symplectic structures.

https://www.math.mcgill.ca/barr/papers/gk.pdf, Grothendieck's Tohoku Paper. Michael Barr's commentary on Grothendieck's landmark 1957 paper introducing abelian categories, derived functors $R^i F$, injective resolutions, sheaf cohomology, and the Grothendieck spectral sequence. Establishes the foundations for Serre duality and Ext functors of sheaves.

https://wstein.org/edu/Fall2003/252/references/ribet-stein-serre/ribet-stein.pdf, Lectures on Serre's Modular Invariance Conjectures. Ribet and Stein's exposition on Serre's modularity conjecture linking odd, irreducible Galois representations $\rho: \operatorname{Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q}) \to \mathrm{GL}_2(\mathbb{F}_p)$ to modular forms of specific weight and level.

https://mathoverflow.net/questions/1151/sheaf-cohomology-and-injective-resolutions, Injective Resolutions in Sheaf Cohomology. Comparison between computing sheaf cohomology using acyclic resolutions (e.g., flasque, soft, fine, or Čech on affine covers) and universal injective resolutions.

https://www.numdam.org/item/10.1007/BF02684688.pdf, Nilpotent Connections and Picard-Fuchs Equations. Nick Katz's classic paper on regular singular differential equations, Gauss–Manin connections, and $p$-adic nilpotence.

https://achinger.impan.pl/fac/fac.pdf, Serre's Faisceaux Algébriques Cohérents (FAC). Annotated translation of Serre's 1955 paper developing coherent sheaves $\mathcal{O}_X$-modules and Čech cohomology on algebraic varieties under the Zariski topology.

https://www.numdam.org/item/CM_1974__28_3_287_0.pdf, Transversality of a General Translate. Kleiman's 12-page paper on the transversality of general translates in Schubert calculus and enumerative geometry over algebraically closed fields in characteristic zero.

https://web.math.princeton.edu/~seri/papers/gowers-Aug4-2006.pdf, Introduction to Nonlinear PDEs. Sergiu Klainerman's comprehensive 72-page introduction to modern nonlinear wave equations and evolutionary PDEs.

https://www.math.stonybrook.edu/robert.lazarsfeld/Reprints/Lazarsfeld.BNP.pdf, Brill-Noether Theory on K3 Surfaces. Robert Lazarsfeld's 10-page proof of the Brill–Noether theorem and Gieseker's theorem using vector bundles on $K3$ surfaces with $\operatorname{Pic}(X) \cong \mathbb{Z}$.

https://www.dam.brown.edu/people/mumford/alg_geom/papers/1983b--EnumGeomModuli-NC.pdf, Enumerative Geometry of Moduli Spaces of Curves. David Mumford's 58-page paper constructing the Chow ring $A^*(\overline{\mathcal{M}}_g)$ of the moduli space of stable curves, defining tautological classes $\kappa_i, \psi_i, \lambda_i$, and applying Grothendieck–Riemann–Roch.

https://thomaskwaring.github.io/gaga.pdf, Serre's GAGA Translation. English translation of Serre's 1956 paper Géométrie Algébrique et Géométrie Analytique establishing the equivalence between algebraic coherent sheaves and analytic coherent sheaves on projective complex varieties.

https://www.math.ucla.edu/~tao/247a.1.06f/, Terence Tao's Harmonic Analysis Notes. Course materials covering Calderón–Zygmund theory, interpolation, singular integrals, and oscillatory integrals.

https://www.math.ucla.edu/~tao/247a.1.06f/notes1.pdf, Calderon-Zygmund-Stein Theory. Tao's notes on classical Calderón–Zygmund theory, kernel estimates, and singular integral bounds on $L^p$ spaces.

https://www.math.ucla.edu/~tao/247a.1.06f/notes2.pdf, Complex and Real Interpolation. Exposition of the Riesz–Thorin complex interpolation theorem, Marcinkiewicz real interpolation, and bounds on linear operators.

https://www.math.ucla.edu/~tao/247a.1.06f/notes3.pdf, Hardy-Littlewood Maximal Inequality. Detailed treatment of the Hardy–Littlewood maximal operator $Mf(x)$, weak $(1,1)$ bounds, and $L^p$ maximal inequalities.

https://www.math.ucla.edu/~tao/247a.1.06f/notes4.pdf, The Hilbert Transform. Theory of the Hilbert transform, singular integral operators failing on the diagonal, and principal value integrals.

https://www.math.ucla.edu/~tao/247a.1.06f/notes5.pdf, Weighted Norm Inequalities. Muckenhoupt $A_p$ weights, weighted $L^p$ estimates, and boundedness of maximal operators.

https://www.math.ucla.edu/~tao/247a.1.06f/notes0.pdf, Introduction to Fourier Summation. Motivating harmonic analysis through convergence of Fourier series and $L^p$ norm interpolation between extreme bounds.

https://www.math.ucla.edu/%7Etao/247b.1.07w/notes6.pdf, Paraproducts and Bilinear Operators. Analysis of multilinear operators, Coifman–Meyer paraproducts, and pointwise nonlinear estimates.

https://www.math.ucla.edu/%7Etao/247b.1.07w/notes7.pdf, The T(1) Theorem. David and Journé's $T(1)$ theorem for $L^2$-boundedness of non-convolution Calderón–Zygmund operators via paraproducts and BMO estimates.

https://www.math.ucla.edu/%7Etao/247b.1.07w/notes8.pdf, Oscillatory Integrals. Stationary phase methods, van der Corput lemma, and asymptotic estimation of oscillatory integrals.

https://www.math.ucla.edu/%7Etao/247b.1.07w/notes9.pdf, Harmonic Analysis on Finite Abelian Groups. Discrete Fourier transforms, Pontryagin duality for finite groups, and convolution identities without measure-theoretic subtleties.

https://www.math.ucla.edu/%7Etao/247b.1.07w/notes10.pdf, Representation Theory of Finite Non-Abelian Groups. Noncommutative harmonic analysis, character theory, matrix coefficients, and Fourier inversion for finite non-abelian groups.

https://www.math.ucla.edu/~tao/247b.1.07w/, Advanced Harmonic Analysis. Course archive covering Littlewood–Paley theory, BMO spaces, and dispersive PDE techniques.

https://www.math.mcgill.ca/darmon/courses/16-17/mf/tate.pdf, Tate's Arithmetic of Elliptic Curves. Henri Darmon's notes based on Tate's classic lectures on elliptic curves, group laws, and formal groups.

https://mathweb.ucsd.edu/~csorense/teaching/math205/Tate_Weil.pdf, Number Theoretic Background for Tate's Thesis. Claus Sorensen's notes on local fields, Haar measures, and Tate's Fourier-analytic reformulation of zeta functions.

https://projecteuclid.org/journals/journal-of-differential-geometry/volume-17/issue-4/Supersymmetry-and-Morse-theory/10.4310/jdg/1214437492.pdf, Supersymmetry and Morse Theory. Edward Witten's paper deriving the Morse inequalities through perturbations $d_t = e^{-th} d e^{th}$ of the de Rham complex and supersymmetric quantum mechanics.

https://archive.ymsc.tsinghua.edu.cn/pacm_download/355/9118-bogomolov.pdf, Equidistribution and the Bogomolov Conjecture. Shou-Wu Zhang's paper proving the Bogomolov conjecture for curves via arithmetic intersection theory and Arakelov heights.

https://webhomes.maths.ed.ac.uk/~v1ranick/papers/milnstas.pdf, Characteristic Classes by Milnor and Stasheff. Classic textbook on vector bundles, Stiefel–Whitney classes, Chern classes, Pontryagin classes, and the Euler class.

https://webhomes.maths.ed.ac.uk/~v1ranick/papers/milnmors.pdf, Morse Theory by John Milnor. Classic text on critical point theory, handlebody attachments, and the topology of smooth manifolds.

https://www.ux1.eiu.edu/~cidelman/Classes/4855%20and%205220/Supplementary%20Texts/MilnorTopDiffVpt.pdf, Topology from the Differentiable Viewpoint. Milnor's text covering smooth maps, regular values, Sard's theorem, Brouwer degree, and the Hopf degree theorem.

https://www.math.purdue.edu/~jlipman/MA598/Serre-Course%20in%20Arithmetic.pdf, Serre's A Course in Arithmetic. Classic introduction to finite fields, $p$-adic fields, quadratic forms, the Hasse–Minkowski theorem, Dirichlet $L$-series, and modular forms for $\mathrm{SL}_2(\mathbb{Z})$.

https://www.math.auckland.ac.nz/~hekmati/Books/BottTu.pdf, Differential Forms in Algebraic Topology. Bott and Tu's book covering de Rham cohomology, Mayer–Vietoris sequences, Poincaré duality, the Thom isomorphism, and spectral sequences.

https://static1.squarespace.com/static/57bf2a6de3df281593b7f57d/t/600db4f1676f6519410f71e9/1611511026377/serre_1972_translation.pdf, Galois Representations of Elliptic Curves. English translation of Serre's 1972 paper on the open image theorem for $\ell$-adic representations $\operatorname{Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q}) \to \mathrm{GL}_2(\mathbb{Z}_\ell)$ of non-CM elliptic curves.

https://www.college-de-france.fr/media/jean-pierre-serre/UPL9140953003901187132_14___Lectures_on_N_X.pdf, Lectures on N(X). Serre's lectures on counting rational points on algebraic varieties over finite fields, exponential sums, and Chebyshev-type bounds.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.26269, The Six-Functor Formalism on Infinity-Categories. Scholze's paper establishing the six-functor formalism as a lax symmetric monoidal functor from a category of correspondences $\operatorname{Corr}(\mathcal{C}, E)$ to $\operatorname{Cat}_\infty$. Formulates pushforward $f_*$, proper pushforward $f_!$, pullback $f^*$, exceptional pullback $f^!$, tensor product $\otimes$, and internal $\operatorname{\mathcal{R}Hom}$. Deduces proper base change and the projection formula $f_!(A \otimes f^*B) \cong f_!(A) \otimes B$ using Lurie's straightening/unstraightening for Cartesian fibrations over $\operatorname{Fin}_*$. Discusses cohomological smoothness, local Verdier duality, and base change for rigid-analytic and perfectoid spaces.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2105.13028, De Rham Cohomology on the Fargues-Fontaine Curve. Studies the de Rham period map and vector bundles on the Fargues–Fontaine curve in $p$-adic Hodge theory.

https://www.nieuwarchief.nl/serie5/pdf/naw5-2010-11-4-250.pdf, Interview with Alain Connes. Reflections on noncommutative geometry, quantum mechanics, and mathematical research philosophy.

http://denise.vella.chemla.free.fr/interview-EMS-2008.pdf, Connes EMS Interview. Discussion of noncommutative geometry, the Riemann hypothesis, the Triangle of Thoughts, and the culture of contemporary mathematics.

https://kssarkaria.org/Quotations%20Sources/ConnesInteng.pdf, Connes on Mathematical Discovery. Alain Connes on the importance of grounding concepts in computation, exploring non-invariant structures, and organizing mathematical ideas conceptually.

https://denisevellachemla.eu/mai10.pdf, Independent Problem Solving. Alain Connes on learning mathematics independently, deep reflection, and challenging existing mathematical dogma.

http://www.ipm.ac.ir/IPM/news/connes-interview.pdf, Plant Analogies and Mental Imagery. Interview on Kiyoshi Oka's transplantation metaphor for mathematical analogies and the role of mental imagery in noncommutative geometry.

https://webhomes.maths.ed.ac.uk/~v1ranick/papers/hopksing.pdf, Quadratic Forms in Homotopy Theory. Hopkins and Singer on quadratic functions in differential cohomology, refined invariants, and generalized differential characters.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0007070, Universal Homotopy Theories. Dugger's paper constructing universal model categories from simplicial presheaves on small categories via homotopy colimits.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1311.3188, Differential Cohomology as Sheaves of Spectra. Bunke, Nikolaus, and Völkl's formulation of differential generalized cohomology theories as sheaves of spectra over the site of smooth manifolds.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1510.06366, Cohomology Operations in Differential Cohomology. Grady and Sati on primary and secondary cohomology operations in differential cohomology via higher stacks.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1604.05988, Differential Cohomology via Stacks. Geometric description of differential cohomology theories, higher gauge fields, and primary operations using smooth $\infty$-stacks.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2312.14338v1, Introduction to Differential Cohomology. Arun Debray's 20-page primer on differential cohomology $\widehat{H}^k(M; \mathbb{Z})$, Cheeger–Simons differential characters, characteristic classes, Chern–Weil theory, and connections on line bundles with physical applications to gauge theory.

https://www.imath.kiev.ua/~mariyka/publ/BPPune-slides-1.pdf, Cohomology and Congruences. Slides on modular congruences, Galois representations, and group cohomology computations.

https://albanymath.github.io/comper25/Slides/vr_st.pdf, Persistence and Steenrod Operations. Applications of the Steenrod algebra and stable cohomology operations to persistent homology in topological data analysis.

https://topos.institute/events/topos-colloquium/slides/2022-06-02.pdf, Torsors, Topoi, and Sheaf Cohomology. Introduction to global elements, $G$-torsors, and classifying topoi. Classifies $H^1(X, G)$ as isomorphism classes of $G$-torsors and $H^2$ as sheaves of groupoids/gerbes, with connections to homotopy type theory.

https://jpascale.web.illinois.edu/lecture1.pdf, Fukaya Categories: Lecture 1. Introduction to symplectic manifolds, Lagrangian submanifolds, and intersection theory in Floer homology.

https://jpascale.web.illinois.edu/lecture2.pdf, Fukaya Categories: Lecture 2. Moduli spaces of pseudo-holomorphic discs, Gromov compactness, and the definition of Floer cohomology.

https://jpascale.web.illinois.edu/lecture3.pdf, Fukaya Categories: Lecture 3. Construction of the $A_\infty$-algebra structure on Lagrangian Floer complexes and higher composition operations $m_k$.

https://jpascale.web.illinois.edu/courses/2018/595/, Homological Mirror Symmetry Course. Introduction to Kontsevich's Homological Mirror Symmetry conjecture relating $D^b \operatorname{Coh}(X)$ and the Fukaya category $\operatorname{Fuk}(X^\vee)$.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/9910179, Introduction to A-Infinity Algebras. Bernhard Keller's 30-page introduction to $A_\infty$-algebras, $A_\infty$-categories, modules, and their derived categories.

https://categorified.net/ASQC4.pdf, Symmetry-Protected Topological Phases. Generalized cohomology theories, cobordism classifications, and topological phases of matter.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/hep-th/0205002, Gerbes and Electromagnetic Duality. Formulating abelian gauge theory and electric-magnetic duality using bundle gerbes and Deligne cohomology.

https://sites.math.washington.edu//~julia/Slides/Evans08.pdf, Cohomological Support Varieties. Julia Pevtsova's slides on support varieties, rank varieties, and modular representation theory of finite groups.

https://math.berkeley.edu/~ltomczak/notes/Lent2023/GrpCohom_Notes.pdf, Notes on Group Cohomology. 38-page lecture notes covering group rings $\mathbb{Z}[G]$, projective resolutions, chain complexes, derived functors $\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathbb{Z}[G]}^*(\mathbb{Z}, M)$, and low-degree cohomology.

https://sudo-rushil.github.io/chroma2022/assets/slides/talk5.pdf, The Steenrod Algebra and Hopf Algebras. Stable cohomology operations, the Steenrod squares $\text{Sq}^k$, and the dual Steenrod algebra as a Hopf algebra.

https://eprints.maths.manchester.ac.uk/1709/1/PRESENTATION.pdf, Computations in the Steenrod Algebra. 14-page presentation detailing the Adem relations, Milnor basis, and Cartan formula.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/0903.4997, Non-Technical Introduction to the Steenrod Algebra. Conceptual explanation of the total Steenrod operation as a perturbation of the Frobenius map in topology.

https://math.gmu.edu/~rallen2/slides/gmu/Multiplication%20Operators%20on%20the%20Bloch%20Space%20of%20the%20Unit%20Disk.pdf, Operators on the Bloch Space. Boundedness and isometries of multiplication and composition operators on Hardy spaces $H^p$ and the Bloch space $\mathcal{B}$ of the open unit disk.

https://math.gmu.edu/~rallen2/slides/gmu/Glimpse%20into%20Operator%20Theory%20I.pdf, Introduction to Operator Theory I. Survey of Banach spaces, Hilbert spaces, bounded linear operators, and adjoints.

https://math.gmu.edu/~rallen2/slides/gmu/Glimpse%20into%20Operator%20Theory%20II.pdf, Introduction to Operator Theory II. Spectral theory of linear operators, point spectrum, continuous spectrum, and resolvent operators.

https://webhomes.maths.ed.ac.uk/~v1ranick/slides/ctcwsli.pdf, Oriented Cobordism Ring. Ranicki's slides on cobordism classes of closed oriented $m$-manifolds, disjoint unions, and the oriented cobordism ring $\Omega_*^{\text{SO}}$.

https://www.ms.u-tokyo.ac.jp/~yasuyuki/yamashita-crest2022-3.pdf, Differential Cohomology and Geometric Quantization. Refining generalized cohomology with smooth differential forms to capture gauge holonomies and Chern–Simons invariants. Covers $H^2(X, \mathbb{Z})$ as isomorphism classes of Hermitian line bundles with $U(1)$-connections, Dirac charge quantization, Cheeger–Simons differential characters, and secondary intersection pairings.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1208.3961, Differential Cohomology via Sheaves of Spectra. Comprehensive survey defining differential cohomology theories as objects in the $\infty$-category $\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{Mfld}, \operatorname{Sp})$ of sheaves of spectra on smooth manifolds. Includes derived categories $\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{Z})$ and recollement theory.

https://math.mit.edu/juvitop/pastseminars/notes_2019_Fall/9-4-19.pdf, Short Overview of Differential Cohomology. Peter Haine's 7-page notes on the axiomatic definition and geometric models of differential cohomology.

https://adebray.github.io/papers/diffcoh.pdf, Comprehensive Differential Cohomology. Arun Debray's 200+ page monograph on Simons–Sullivan differential characters, generalized differential cohomology, and topological field theories.

https://www.imo.universite-paris-saclay.fr/~luc.illusie/Kan-Lectures-Illusie-A.pdf, Lectures on the de Rham Complex. Luc Illusie's 66 slides on algebraic de Rham cohomology, the de Rham–Witt complex, and crystalline cohomology.

https://mgallauer.warwick.ac.uk/docs/m6ff.pdf, Foundations of Six-Functor Formalisms. Martin Gallauer's monograph explaining the categorical framework of the six functors ($f^*, f_*, f_!, f^!, \otimes, \underline{\operatorname{Hom}}$) on coefficient systems. Formulates Grothendieck duality, relative purity, proper and smooth base change, localization exact triangles, and $\mathbb{A}^1$-homotopy invariance. Discusses constructible $\ell$-adic sheaves, holonomic $\mathcal{D}$-modules, and the categorical representation of homology and cohomology via the tensor unit $\mathbf{1}$.

https://epub.uni-regensburg.de/31403/6/final.pdf, Fourier-Mukai Transforms and Push-Pull Duality. Derived push-pull integral transforms $\Phi_{\mathcal{P}}(\mathcal{F}) = \mathbf{R}q_*(\mathcal{P} \otimes^{\mathbf{L}} p^*\mathcal{F})$ between derived categories of coherent sheaves and their extensions to $T^n$-principal bundles.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1312.7164, Integral Transforms for Coherent Sheaves. David Nadler's paper on integral transforms, categorical convolution, and geometric representation theory.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/0805.0157, Integral Transforms and Drinfeld Centers. Ben-Zvi, Francis, and Nadler on integral transforms for categorical sheaves and the character theory of Drinfeld centers.

https://www.ms.uky.edu/~guillou/F20/751Notes.pdf, Equivariant Homotopy and Cohomology. 68-page lecture notes by Bert Guillou on $G$-spaces, equivariant homotopy groups, Bredon cohomology, and equivariant spectra.

https://guests.mpim-bonn.mpg.de/ferdinand/q-deRham.pdf, Introduction to q-de Rham Cohomology. Ferdinand Wagner's exposition of $q$-de Rham cohomology, $q$-connections, and Habiro rings.

https://www.uni-muenster.de/IVV5WS/WebHop/user/nikolaus/Papers/sheaves-on-manifolds.pdf, Sheaves on Manifolds and Efimov K-Theory. Nikolaus's paper constructing six-functor formalisms on $\operatorname{Shv}(X; \mathcal{D}(\mathbb{Z}))$ for locally compact Hausdorff spaces via compactly assembled presentable stable $\infty$-categories and Efimov's continuous $K$-theory. Details dualizable categories, rigid monoidal categories, Frobenius algebras, and self-duality of $\operatorname{Shv}(X; \operatorname{Sp})$.

https://www.uni-muenster.de/IVV5WS/WebHop/user/nikolaus/Recordings%20Surgery/Surgery.pdf, Algebraic Surgery over the Integers. Lecture notes on algebraic surgery theory and Gromov–Witten invariants over $\operatorname{Spec} \mathbb{Z}$.

https://www.uni-muenster.de/IVV5WS/WebHop/user/nikolaus/Files/ICM_Slides.pdf, Frobenius Homomorphisms in Higher Algebra. Thomas Nikolaus's ICM slides on topological Hochschild homology (THH), topological cyclic homology (TC), and cyclotomic spectra.

https://www.uni-muenster.de/IVV5WS/WebHop/user/nikolaus/Files/Algebraic%20Geometry.pdf, Algebraic Geometry I-III. Nikolaus's lecture notes covering schemes, sheaves, cohomology, and moduli theory.

https://people.mpim-bonn.mpg.de/scholze/Gestalten.pdf, Gestalten and Stefanich Rings. Scholze's 90-page paper on Gestalten and the geometry of Stefanich rings. Develops higher Tannakian duality for symmetric monoidal presentable categories $\operatorname{Mod}(A)$, $\kappa$-compactly generated categories, and affine stacks. Explores $n$-affineness, non-flat descent, and categorified analytic geometry in $\operatorname{CAlg}(\operatorname{Pr}_{\text{st}}^L)$.

https://sites.math.washington.edu//~hart/m556/, Lectures on Banach Spaces. Lecture notes on functional analysis, dual spaces, weak topologies, and spectral theory.

https://math.mit.edu/~hrm/papers/ss.pdf, Origins of Sheaf Theory. Historical account of the development of sheaves by Leray, Cartan, Serre, and Grothendieck.

https://arminstraub.com/math/what-is-column, AMS What Is...? Column Collection. Expository articles covering foundational mathematical concepts.

https://www.fields.utoronto.ca/programs/scientific/08-09/o-minimal/miniworkshops/Prelli.pdf, Sobolev Sheaves and Subanalytic Sites. Luca Prelli's 99 slides developing sheaf theory on subanalytic and o-minimal sites. Covers the DTOP site, tempered holomorphic functions, ultrafilter compactifications, the spectral topology on fibres, and the six-functor formalism for subanalytic sheaves.

https://ncatlab.org/nlab/files/BhattAlmostRing.pdf, Concise Introduction to Almost Ring Theory. Bhargav Bhatt's notes on Faltings' almost mathematics over a perfectoid field $K$. Studies the valuation ring $K^\circ$ with idempotent maximal ideal $\mathfrak{m} = \mathfrak{m}^2$. The category of almost modules $K^{\circ a}\text{-}\mathbf{Mod} = K^\circ\text{-}\mathbf{Mod} / \Sigma$ is obtained by localizing at the Serre subcategory $\Sigma$ of $\mathfrak{m}$-torsion modules. Covers almost flatness, almost projective modules, diagonal idempotents, almost étale morphisms, and deformation theory via the almost purity theorem.

https://www.math.harvard.edu/media/Narayanan-Modular-Forms-Thesis.pdf, L-Functions and Partition Functions. Thesis exploring the connections between partition functions, modular forms, and thermodynamics.

https://www.rnta.eu/Nesin2017/PV-lecture1.pdf, Introduction to L-Functions. PV's slides covering Dirichlet series $D_f(s) = \sum f(n)n^{-s}$, Dirichlet characters, conductors, local Euler factors, completed $L$-functions, and the Generalized Riemann Hypothesis.

https://www.rnta.eu/Nesin2017/PV-lecture2.pdf, Dedekind Zeta Functions and Number Fields. Covers the Dedekind zeta function $\zeta_K(s) = \sum_{\mathfrak{a}} N(\mathfrak{a})^{-s}$, the analytic class number formula, Dirichlet's unit theorem, decomposition groups $D_{\mathfrak{p}}$, inertia groups $I_{\mathfrak{p}}$, and Frobenius automorphisms.

https://webusers.imj-prg.fr/~michel.waldschmidt/articles/pdf/ZetaRiemann.pdf, Tutorial on the Riemann Zeta Function. Michel Waldschmidt's 192-page comprehensive tutorial on the analytic properties, special values, and zeros of $\zeta(s)$.

https://www.rnta.eu/Nesin2017/PV-lecture3.pdf, Artin and Hasse-Weil L-Functions. Slides on Artin $L$-functions attached to Galois representations, Artin's conjecture, and local Euler factors of algebraic varieties.

https://www.rnta.eu/Nesin2017/PV-lecture4.pdf, The Selberg Class and Langlands Philosophy. Overview of the Selberg class of $L$-functions, functional equations, automorphic representations, and the Langlands correspondence.

https://www.rnta.eu/Nesin2017/PSANT.pdf, Pierre Samuel's Algebraic Theory of Numbers. Text on Dedekind domains, ideal class groups, prime ideal factorizations, and the geometry of numbers.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2509.21672, An L-Function Approach to Class Field Theory. 101-page monograph formulating global and local class field theory through the analytic properties and factorization of abelian $L$-functions.

https://people.mpim-bonn.mpg.de/scholze/Almost.pdf, Notes on Almost Mathematics. Peter Scholze's condensed notes on almost ring theory, almost étale algebras, and their role in the foundations of perfectoid geometry.

https://www.ihes.fr/~/gabber/t-str.pdf, Notes on t-Structures and Constructibility. Ofer Gabber's notes on constructible sheaves, perverse sheaves, and $t$-structures on derived categories over schemes.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1108.6020, Grothendieck-Verdier Duality and Autonomous Categories. Boyarchenko and Drinfeld on *-autonomous categories, Grothendieck–Verdier categories, and dualizing objects in constructible derived categories.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1011.2189, Representability of Derived Stacks. J.P. Pridham's criteria for representability of derived Artin stacks and formal moduli problems via deformation theory and simplicial rings.

https://www2.cs.uh.edu/~rizk/cosc2430/slides/04-LinAlgTutorial.pdf, Numerical Linear Algebra Tutorial. 235 slides reviewing vector spaces, matrix factorizations (LU, QR, SVD), and iterative linear solvers.

https://web.stanford.edu/~boyd/vmls/vmls-slides.pdf, Applied Linear Algebra by Stephen Boyd. Slides covering vectors, matrices, least squares, QR factorization, and symmetric eigenvalue decompositions.

https://www.mathsoc.jp/assets/pdf/activity/video/2023autumn/collins_20230921-slide.pdf, Random Matrix Theory and Free Probability. Benoît Collins's 79 slides surveying Gaussian ensembles, Haar unitary matrices, and free probability.

https://course-repos.math.gatech.edu/math1553/slides/allslides-web.pdf, Linear Algebra Course Slides. 501 slides covering systems of linear equations, eigenspaces, orthogonality, and the spectral theorem.

https://icml.cc/media/icml-2021/Slides/10840_enEDO6J.pdf, Random Matrix Theory for Machine Learning. ICML tutorial on high-dimensional random matrix spectral properties and spectral clustering.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1507.06867, Derived Induction and Restriction. Matthew Ando and Justin Noel on derived induction, equivariant cohomology, and transfer maps in chromatic homotopy theory.

https://www.nullplug.org/talks/Banff-Noel.pdf, Derived Induction in Equivariant Homotopy. Justin Noel's slides on derived induction theorems and transfer arguments for equivariant ring spectra.

https://www.nullplug.org/talks/Noel-Banff-AR.pdf, The Transfer Argument in Stable Homotopy. Slides on Artin–Tate motives, the transfer map, and Adams–Novikov spectral sequences.

https://aimath.org/wp-content/uploads/bristol-2018-slides/Conrey-talk.pdf, L-Functions and Random Matrix Theory. Brian Conrey's talk on the distribution of zeros of $L$-functions and eigenvalue statistics of the Circular Unitary Ensemble (CUE).

https://sanathdevalapurkar.github.io/files/dag-IZ-tmf.pdf, Anderson Duality for Derived Stacks. Sanath Devalapurkar's notes on Anderson duality, topological modular forms ($\operatorname{tmf}$), and the Brown–Comenetz dual of ring spectra.

https://indico.math.cnrs.fr/event/10477/attachments/5296/8775/Course_Zhenyu_LIAO.pdf, Random Matrix Theory Foundations. Zhenyu Liao's lecture series on Marchenko–Pastur distributions, resolvent methods, and concentration.

https://www.ee.cuhk.edu.hk/~wkma/engg5781/, Matrix Computations Course. Course homepage covering matrix decompositions, Rayleigh quotients, and convex optimization.

https://faculty.ucmerced.edu/mhyang/course/eecs275/, Matrix Computation Slides. Lecture slides on advanced numerical linear algebra, low-rank approximations, and spectral graph theory.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0002064, Almost Ring Theory. Gabber and Ramero's 300+ page foundational treatise on almost ring theory, non-flat descent, cotangent complexes, and $p$-adic Hodge theory.

https://web.ma.utexas.edu/users/dafr/CBMS133.pdf, Topology in Quantum Field Theory. Dan Freed's CBMS lectures on topological quantum field theories, bordism categories, and characteristic classes.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1105.3968, Anderson Duality in Stable Homotopy Theory. Stojanoska's paper computing Anderson duality for the spectrum of topological modular forms $\operatorname{tmf}[1/2]$ as a 21-fold suspension. Connects formal groups, Morava $K$-theories, and Deligne–Mumford moduli stacks of elliptic curves.

https://www.math.mcgill.ca/barr/papers/scatll.pdf, Star-Autonomous Categories and the Chu Construction. Michael Barr's paper on constructing *-autonomous categories via the Chu construction over cartesian closed categories.

https://www.math.mcgill.ca/barr/papers/chu-hist.pdf, History of the Chu Construction. Barr's survey on the origin of *-autonomous categories, linear logic, and duality in computer science and category theory.

https://people.math.harvard.edu/~lurie/papers/DAG-XIV.pdf, Representability Theorems in Derived Geometry. Jacob Lurie's DAG XIV establishing representability criteria for derived Artin stacks and dualizing modules.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2310.12456, Introduction to Algebraic Stacks. Adeel Khan's notes on simplicial sets, Kan horn filling conditions, $\infty$-categories, sheaves, stacks, and descent via totalization.

https://math.bme.hu/~gabor/oktatas/SztoM/TaoVu.AddComb.pdf, Additive Combinatorics. Terence Tao and Van Vu's monograph on sumset estimates, Freiman's theorem, Szemerédi's regularity lemma, and the polynomial method.

https://math.bme.hu/~gabor/PGG.pdf, Probability and Geometry on Groups. Gábor Pete's book on random walks on groups, percolation, expander graphs, and geometric group theory.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2010.09216, Compact Closed Categories in Homotopical Algebra. Survey of compact closed and *-autonomous categories, dualizable objects, and their classification in homotopical algebra.

https://people.math.harvard.edu/~landesman/assets/class-groups-notes.pdf, Distribution of Class Groups. Landesman's notes on the Cohen–Lenstra heuristics, Minkowski bounds, $\Gamma$-Hurwitz spaces, and the Grothendieck–Lefschetz trace formula over $\mathbb{F}_q(t)$.

https://www.linux.bucknell.edu/~pm040/MySlides/pomona.pdf, Algebraic Combinatorics and Symmetric Functions. Slides covering Schur functions, Young tableaux, the Littlewood–Richardson rule, Schubert calculus, and 3-point Gromov–Witten invariants.

https://www.math.uni-bonn.de/people/scholze/Condensed.pdf, Lectures on Condensed Mathematics. Peter Scholze's foundational notes developing condensed sets, condensed abelian groups, solid abelian groups, and analytic rings. Proves generation by compact projectives via Stone–Čech compactifications of profinite sets.

https://www.math.columbia.edu/~calebji/6_functors.pdf, Guidelines for the Six-Functor Formalism. Caleb Ji's summary of Scholze's 6-functor formalism on stable $\infty$-categories of sheaves, base change, projection formulas, and Verdier duality.

https://user.math.uzh.ch/ayoub/PDF-Files/AGV.pdf, Six-Functor Formalism for Motives. Joseph Ayoub's treatise on the six-functor formalism in motivic homotopy theory and cross-functors.

https://lebras.perso.math.cnrs.fr/Notes_Dauser.pdf, Six Functors for Analytic Stacks. Dauser's 13-page summary of the six-functor formalism for $p$-adic analytic stacks.

https://oscarharr.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/04/verdier-duality.pdf, Verdier Duality in Topology. 20-page expository notes on Verdier duality, compact support pushforward $f_!$, and exceptional pullback $f^!$.

https://dagur.sites.ku.dk/files/2022/01/condensed-foundations.pdf, Foundations of Condensed Mathematics. Dagur Asgeirsson's 100-page master's thesis providing an accessible introduction to condensed sets, solid modules, and liquid vector spaces.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2412.15780, Uniqueness of Six-Functor Formalisms. Establishes the axiomatic uniqueness and rigidity of the six-functor formalism on geometric categories.

https://www.seas.upenn.edu/~jean/sheaves-cohomology.pdf, Homological Algebra and Sheaves. Jean Gallier's 600-page comprehensive treatise on homological algebra, sheaf cohomology, and spectral sequences.

https://server.mcm.ac.cn/~zheng/LI/Laumon.pdf, Lusztig Induction and Restriction. Gérard Laumon's slides on character sheaves, Deligne–Lusztig induction, and representations of finite reductive groups.

https://server.mcm.ac.cn/~zheng/LI/Temkin.pdf, Logarithmic Geometry and Desingularization. Michael Temkin's slides on logarithmic geometry, canonical resolution of singularities, and functorial desingularization.

https://janschuetz.perso.math.cnrs.fr/skripte/lecture_notes_etale_cohomology.pdf, Étale Cohomology Notes. 93-page comprehensive course notes covering étale sites, proper base change, and the Weil conjectures.

https://janschuetz.perso.math.cnrs.fr/skripte/lecture_notes_lubin_tate.pdf, Lubin-Tate Theory Notes. 141-page lecture notes on formal groups, Lubin–Tate deformation spaces, and local class field theory.

https://janschuetz.perso.math.cnrs.fr/skripte/lecture_notes_arithlang.pdf, Arithmetic Langlands Notes. 103-page course notes on automorphic forms, Galois representations, and the arithmetic Langlands correspondence.

https://janschuetz.perso.math.cnrs.fr/skripte/vorlesung_the_curve.pdf, The Fargues-Fontaine Curve. 85-page lecture notes on the schematic and adic Fargues–Fontaine curve in $p$-adic Hodge theory.

https://janschuetz.perso.math.cnrs.fr/skripte/homology_profinite.pdf, Solid Group Cohomology. 18-page notes on the condensation functor on topological groups and solid cohomology for locally profinite groups.

https://www.math.uni-bonn.de/people/ja/alggeoI/notes.pdf, Algebraic Geometry I Notes. Peter Scholze's course notes on affine schemes, projective schemes, sheaves, and scheme-theoretic constructions.

http://web.archive.org/web/20190513153411/http://www.math.uni-bonn.de/people/ja/alggeoII/notes_II.pdf, Algebraic Geometry II: Sheaf Cohomology. Scholze's notes covering flat and smooth morphisms, Hilbert polynomials, Serre duality, and the Riemann–Roch theorem for curves.

https://bpb-us-e1.wpmucdn.com/s.wayne.edu/dist/0/60/files/2019/11/Beaudry-PartI-annotated.pdf, Chromatic Homotopy Theory I. Agnès Beaudry's introduction to complex cobordism $\operatorname{MU}$, formal group laws, and the Adams–Novikov spectral sequence.

https://bpb-us-e1.wpmucdn.com/s.wayne.edu/dist/0/60/files/2019/11/Beaudry-PartII-annotated.pdf, Chromatic Homotopy Theory II. Morava $K$-theories $K(n)$, Morava $E$-theories $E_n$, and the chromatic filtration.

https://bpb-us-e1.wpmucdn.com/s.wayne.edu/dist/0/60/files/2019/11/Beaudry-PartIII-annotated.pdf, Chromatic Homotopy Theory III. The chromatic spectral sequence, chromatic convergence, and the telescope conjecture.

https://mathoverflow.net/questions/38856/jokes-in-the-sense-of-littlewood-examples, Formal Geometric Series in Projective Geometry. Littlewood-style heuristic expansions, such as $\frac{\mathbb{C}^{n+1} \setminus \{0\}}{\mathbb{C} \setminus \{0\}} = \mathbb{C}^n + \dots + \mathbb{C}^1 + 1$ reflecting $\mathbb{P}^n$ cell decompositions.

https://www.cs.cmu.edu/~quake-papers/painless-conjugate-gradient.pdf, Painless Conjugate Gradient Method. Jonathan Shewchuk's classic guide to the conjugate gradient method and optimization of positive-definite quadratic forms.

https://server.mcm.ac.cn/~zheng/slides/compatible_IHES.pdf, Local Monodromy and Compatible Systems. Weizhe Zheng's slides on local monodromy, $\ell$-adic representations, and compatible systems of Galois representations.

http://verbit.ru/MATH/GEOM-2013/pde-en-4.pdf, Sheaves, Germs, and Constant Sheaves. Misha Verbitsky's notes on stalks, germs, direct limits of section modules, and the characterization of constant sheaves.

https://server.mcm.ac.cn/~zheng/slides/nearby-slides.pdf, Vanishing and Nearby Cycles. 54 slides on vanishing cycles $R\Psi$ and $R\Phi$, local systems on punctured discs, and Milnor fibers over general bases.

https://server.mcm.ac.cn/~zheng/slides/PANT2011.pdf, Mod-l Cohomology Algebras. Slides on mod-$\ell$ étale cohomology algebras, cycle classes, and cup product structures.

https://server.mcm.ac.cn/~zheng/slides/ICCM10.pdf, Mod-l Equivariant Cohomology. 43 slides on Cartesian sheaves, equivariant étale cohomology, and Grothendieck topologies.

https://www.math.ias.edu/~bhatt/teaching/mat549f22/lectures.pdf, Prismatic F-Gauges. Bhargav Bhatt's lecture notes on prismatic $F$-gauges, Breuil–Kisin twists, syntomic cohomology, Nygaard filtrations, and topological cyclic homology.

https://math.ucr.edu/home/baez/motives.pdf, John Baez on Motives. Expository notes explaining Grothendieck's motives, Weil cohomology theories, and point-counting on projective spaces.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2412.03382, Berkovich Motives. Peter Scholze's paper on Berkovich spectra, arc sheaves, the arc topology, and the derived category of Berkovich motives $\mathcal{D}_{\text{mot}}(X)$.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2505.24599, Wild Betti Sheaves. Scholze's paper developing the theory of wild Betti sheaves via Verdier quotients, exponential local systems, and the Fourier transform on ring stacks.

https://people.mpim-bonn.mpg.de/zagier/files/doi/10.1007/978-3-540-30308-4_1/fulltext.pdf, Zagier on the Dilogarithm. Don Zagier's survey on the dilogarithm $\operatorname{Li}_2(z)$, the Bloch–Wigner function $D(z)$, hyperbolic 3-manifold volumes, and the Bloch group $B(F)$.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1911.02342, Meromorphic Continuation of Eisenstein Series. Proof of the meromorphic continuation of adelic Eisenstein series using convolution algebras of compactly supported smooth functions and Cramer's rule.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1812.04788, O-Minimal Geometry and Hyperbolic Manifolds. Applications of o-minimal structures, semialgebraic sets, and tame topology to cusp shapes and hyperbolic Dehn fillings.

http://matwbn.icm.edu.pl/ksiazki/bcp/bcp42/bcp42111.pdf, Survey on Dehn Surgery. Survey of Dehn surgery, prime 3-manifold decompositions, and hyperbolic knot complements.

https://www.maths.ed.ac.uk/~v1ranick/books/scm.pdf, Surgery on Compact Manifolds. Wall's foundational book on surgery theory, $L$-groups, and the classification of high-dimensional manifolds.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2308.01670, Affine Homology of Stacks. Defining and computing the affine homology of affine stacks, base change theorems, and dimensional filtrations.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2311.12122, K-Theory of Moduli Stacks. Explicit computations of pushforwards, hyperplane classes, self-intersection formulas, and the localization theorem in the $K$-theory of moduli strata.

https://hal.science/hal-03347136/file/stacks-AM.pdf, Stacks and Perfect Complexes. Explores higher Artin 2-stacks, automorphism groupoids $\mathbb{G}_m \times \mathbb{G}_m \times B\mathbb{G}_a$, and descent conditions for perfect complexes.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0207210, The Resolution Property for Stacks. Burt Totaro's paper characterizing algebraic stacks that admit resolutions of coherent sheaves by vector bundles.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2011.14234, The Tenfold Way and Superalgebras. John Baez on real division algebras ($\mathbb{R}, \mathbb{C}, \mathbb{H}$), super vector spaces, Clifford algebras, and Dyson's tenfold classification of topological matter.

https://webhomes.maths.ed.ac.uk/~v1ranick/papers/deligne.pdf, Spin Groups and Clifford Superalgebras. Deligne's notes on quadratic vector spaces, Spin groups, spinor representations, Bott periodicity modulo 8, and the super Brauer group.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1309.1968, Grothendieck-Teichmuller Theory and Dessins d'Enfants. Survey on Grothendieck's dessins d'enfants, bipartite ribbon graphs, the absolute Galois group $\operatorname{Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathbb{Q})$, and the Grothendieck–Teichmüller group $\widehat{GT}$.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/0903.4067, Drinfeld Associators and Braided Categories. Universal construction of braided monoidal categories from Lie algebras, quasi-Hopf algebras, and solutions to the Kashiwara–Vergne conjecture.

https://qchu.wordpress.com/2020/11/01/meditation-on-the-sylow-theorems-i/, Conceptual Proof of the Sylow Theorems. Qiaochu Yuan's exposition using $p$-group actions on finite sets, orbit-stabilizer relations, and fixed-point congruences $|X^G| \equiv |X| \pmod p$.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2003.06521, Hodge Correlators and Motivic Periods. Alexander Goncharov on Hodge correlators, dilogarithms, the Bloch–Wigner function, and twistor interpretations of Archimedean periods.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2010.07238, Motivic Fundamental Groups of CM Elliptic Curves. Applications of higher-weight Hodge correlators to the motivic fundamental groups of elliptic curves with complex multiplication.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/0803.0297, Hodge Correlators by Goncharov. Foundational paper on Hodge correlators, Feynman integrals on Riemann surfaces, and mixed Hodge structures on fundamental groups.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2503.20906v1, Higher Fukaya Categories and Shifted Symplectic Stacks. Speculative framework for higher Fukaya categories $n\operatorname{Fuk}(X)$ associated with $(n-1)$-shifted symplectic stacks and iterated derived intersections.

https://adebray.github.io/lecture_notes/m390c_GL_2021_notes.pdf, Geometric Langlands Course Notes. Arun Debray's notes covering the spectral dictionary, continuous and discrete spectra of commuting operators, and the geometric Langlands correspondence.

https://people.math.harvard.edu/~auroux/papers/lec-nantes.pdf, Introduction to Fukaya Categories. Denis Auroux's lecture notes on pseudo-holomorphic strips, Lagrangian Floer cohomology, transversality, and $A_\infty$-operations.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2511.22755, Zeta Spectral Triples. Alain Connes on spectral zeta functions, regularized determinants, and non-commutative geometry of the Riemann zeros.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2506.18174, Duality and Linearization for p-Adic Lie Groups. Dustin Clausen's thesis on Lazard–Poincaré duality for compact $p$-adic Lie groups, tangent deformations, and the six-functor formalism on $\mathbb{Q}_p/\mathbb{Q}_p^\times$.

https://www.math.columbia.edu/~chaoli/IHES.pdf, Geometric and Arithmetic Theta Correspondences. Chao Li's IHES lectures on the Kudla program, unitary group cycles, and arithmetic intersection theory.

https://math.dartmouth.edu/~lesen/notes/talk_slides.pdf, Introduction to Shimura Varieties. 17-slide overview of Shimura data, hermitian symmetric domains, and arithmetic quotients.

https://hal.science/hal-04275698/document, Sophie Morel on Shimura Varieties. 65-page survey on the cohomology of Shimura varieties, weighted truncation, and intersection cohomology.

https://www.math.columbia.edu/~chaoli/slides/LeiZhang.pdf, Twisted Automorphic Descent and GGP. Chao Li and Lei Zhang's slides on the Gan–Gross–Prasad conjecture and automorphic descent for classical groups.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1306.2070, Perfectoid Siegel Spaces. Peter Scholze's paper establishing Galois representations associated with the mod-$p$ cohomology of locally symmetric spaces and Siegel modular varieties.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2410.13038, Guide to the Six-Functor Formalism. Lucas Mann's 196-page comprehensive guide to the six-functor formalism on correspondences, Cartesian fibrations, and étale/rigid categories.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2206.02022, Six Functors in Rigid Analytic Geometry. Lucas Mann's monograph developing the 6-functor formalism for $p$-torsion étale sheaves on rigid-analytic and perfectoid spaces.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1410.3870, Matroids and Simplicial Complexes. Pure simplicial complexes, uniform matroids, graphic matroids, and independent sets.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2005.07395, Binary Geometry of Generalized Permutohedra. Associadedra, permutohedra, and scattering amplitude geometry in mathematical physics.

https://www.math.kyoto-u.ac.jp/~iritani/iritani_algsymp2021.pdf, Tropical Geometry and Mirror Symmetry. Hiroshi Iritani's slides on period asymptotics, tropical disks, and mirror symmetry.

https://www.kwlan.org/articles/intro-sh-ex.pdf, Introduction to Shimura Varieties by Kai-Wen Lan. Basic examples of $\mathrm{SL}_2(\mathbb{R})$, Shimura data $(G, X)$, and moduli spaces of abelian varieties with PEL structures.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2409.10557, Stacky Approach to p-Adic Hodge Theory. Formal stacks, de Rham complexes, and Hodge filtrations for group schemes like $\mathbb{G}_a$.

https://www.jmilne.org/math/xnotes/svi.pdf, Milne's Introduction to Shimura Varieties. Foundational notes covering bounded symmetric domains, Siegel modular varieties, and canonical models.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0609184, Convex Polytopes and Braid Cones. Combinatorial dictionary between preposets, braid arrangements, and normal fans of permutohedra.

https://people.math.ethz.ch/~oezlemi/pdf/papers/2018-DIT-KroneckerLF.pdf, Kronecker's First Limit Formula. Derivation and computations for binary quadratic forms and the Dedekind eta function.

https://guests.mpim-bonn.mpg.de/ferdinand/q-Thesis.pdf, Habiro Cohomology and Quantum Invariants. Ferdinand Wagner's PhD thesis on $q$-de Rham cohomology, Habiro rings, hyperbolic knot volumes, and dilogarithms.

https://cmsa.fas.harvard.edu/media/Kim-1-nopause.pdf, Arithmetic Topology and Gauge Theory. Minhyong Kim's slides on the arithmetic dictionary between 3-manifolds and number rings, arithmetic Chern–Simons theory, and path integrals.

https://people.mpim-bonn.mpg.de/zagier/files/doi/10.4171/176-1/33/HirzebruchLectureECM2016.pdf, Arithmetic Topology and Modular Curves. Don Zagier's Hirzebruch Lecture exploring differential equations, Legendre elliptic curves, and topology.

https://www.cip.ifi.lmu.de/~grinberg/algebra/lambda.pdf, Notes on Lambda-Rings. Darij Grinberg's introduction to $\lambda$-rings, Adams operations, representation rings, and symmetric functions.

https://sites.math.duke.edu/~hain/talks/brin_2023.pdf, History of Polylogarithms and Regulators. Richard Hain's slides on polylogarithms, Dirichlet's unit theorem, regulators, and mapping class groups.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2502.10945, Tannakian Categories by J.S. Milne. Comprehensive survey of Tannakian categories, fiber functors, affine group schemes, and fundamental groups.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1605.00929, Classification of Topological Vector Bundles. Homotopy classification of real and complex vector bundles over CW complexes via Grassmannians.

https://www.math.mcgill.ca/darmon/pub/Articles/Research/72.DV2/borcherds.pdf, Borcherds Products and Automorphic Forms. Henri Darmon's exposition on Borcherds products, theta lifts, and singular moduli.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/0708.4401, Theory of 2-Gerbes and Picard Groupoids. Categorical definition of algebraic group scheme gerbes, band structures, and modules over Picard groupoids $\operatorname{Pic}^\mathbb{Z}$.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/math/0605346, Siegel Modular Forms Introduction. Basic theory of the Siegel upper half-plane $\mathbb{H}_g$, the symplectic group $\operatorname{Sp}_{2g}(\mathbb{Z})$, and Siegel modular forms.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/0708.2160, Rational Algebraic K-Theory. Computations and structural results in rational algebraic $K$-theory of schemes and rings.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/0711.2812, Baxter Q-Operators and Quantum Integrable Systems. Connections between Baxter $Q$-operators, quantum groups, and representations of the Weil group.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1904.00179, The Deligne-Ihara Conjecture. Exposition of periods, mixed Tate motives, and the action of the motivic Galois group on the fundamental group of $\mathbb{P}^1 \setminus \{0,1,\infty\}$.

https://peterjhaine.github.io/files/KFF.pdf, Algebraic K-Theory of Finite Fields. Peter Haine's notes on Quillen's computation of $K_*(\mathbb{F}_q)$ via the plus construction $B\mathrm{GL}(\mathbb{F}_q)^+$ and the Adams conjecture.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/0912.2157, Classification of Topological Insulators. Topological states of matter, symmetry classes, and classification via real and complex topological $K$-theory.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1208.5055, Topological K-Theory and Condensed Matter. Dan Freed's lectures on twistings, the tenfold way, and the K-theory classification of topological insulators and superconductors.

https://people.math.harvard.edu/~auroux/papers/slides-horipen.pdf, Lefschetz Pencils and Symplectic 4-Manifolds. Denis Auroux's slides on Lefschetz fibrations, monodromy representations, mapping class groups, and Hurwitz equivalence.

https://indico.math.cnrs.fr/event/7514/contributions/6245/attachments/3393/4540/ARGUZ-slides.pdf, Introduction to Gromov-Witten Theory. Hulya Arguz's slides on moduli of stable maps, Kontsevich's recursive formulas, virtual fundamental classes, and torus localization.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2109.13323, Relative Gromov-Witten Theory. Nodal curve insertions, degeneration formulas, and Gromov–Witten invariants of pairs.

http://www.davidrenshawhansen.net/etcohtalkhandout.pdf, Étale Cohomology of Rigid Analytic Spaces. David Hansen's handout on the history and modern developments of étale cohomology for adic and perfectoid spaces.

https://seminari-simba.github.io/slides/simba211103-DMartinez.pdf, Simplicial Sets and Stable Homotopy Theory. Slides introducing simplicial sets, geometric realizations, spectra, and stable model categories.

https://cms.dm.uba.ar/Members/gcorti/workgroup.GNC/SLIDES%20Arun%20Soor.pdf, Summary of Six-Functor Formalisms. Arun Soor's 23 slides defining the six-functor formalism as a lax symmetric monoidal functor on correspondences $\operatorname{Corr}(\mathcal{C}, E) \to \operatorname{Cat}_\infty$. Covers proper base change, projection formulas, and Verdier duality.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/2301.03821, Poincaré Duality in Six-Functor Formalisms. Formal properties of Poincaré duality, trace maps, and Verdier's diagonal trick within the six-functor context.

https://www.its.caltech.edu/~matilde/ThermoSemirings.pdf, Thermodynamic Semirings and Information. Matilde Marcolli's notes on tropical geometry, semirings, and the algebraic structure of statistical thermodynamics.

https://www.math.mcgill.ca/darmon/courses/18-19/cft/refs/neukirch.pdf, Class Field Theory by Jürgen Neukirch. Comprehensive textbook covering local and global class field theory, Galois cohomology, and abstract class formations.

https://www.imo.universite-paris-saclay.fr/~francois.charles/TLDR9.pdf, Summary of Class Field Theory. François Charles's concise recap of the main theorems of local and global class field theory and the Artin map.

http://mat.msgsu.edu.tr/~aad/2012/Slides/paganin.pdf, Galois Cohomology and Class Field Theory. 33 slides on group cohomology, Tate cohomology, Herbrand quotients, and their role in class field theory.

https://www.samraskin.net/cft/notes.pdf, Course Notes on Class Field Theory. Sam Raskin's 119-page lecture notes covering local fields, adèles, idèles, and the cohomological proofs of class field theory.

https://people.math.harvard.edu/~hamann/Course_Notes_CFT_Week_11.pdf, Galois Cohomology and Class Formations. Hamann's notes on abstract class formations, the Tate–Nakayama theorem, and reciprocity maps.

https://yhzhumath.github.io/ANT%20notes.pdf, Advanced Number Theory and Class Field Theory. 85-page notes covering ray class groups, $L$-functions, and global reciprocity.

https://iwoat.github.io/2018/slides/Ravenel.pdf, Foundations of Stable Homotopy Theory. Hill, Hopkins, and Ravenel's slides on spectra, model categories, Bousfield localization, symmetric spectra, Day convolution, and smash products.

https://arxiv.org/pdf/1901.07305, Derived Categories of Coherent Sheaves. Introduction to bounded derived categories $\mathcal{D}^b(\operatorname{Coh} X)$, standard exact sequences (Euler, ideal, normal), and Fourier–Mukai transforms.

https://mathoverflow.net/questions/97361/explaining-mukai-fourier-transforms-physically, Physical Heuristics for Fourier-Mukai Transforms. Discussion on Fourier–Mukai transforms as generalized T-duality, mapping vector bundles to skyscraper sheaves in dual abelian varieties.

https://richardwong.rice.edu/assets/slides/NishidaNilpotenceTalk.pdf, The Nishida Nilpotence Theorem. Richard Wong's slides on the ring structure of stable homotopy groups of spheres $\pi_*(\mathbb{S})$ and the nilpotence of positive-degree elements.

https://www.math.tau.ac.il/~sarabt/zoominar/slides/231117_Manolescu.pdf, Grid Homology and Floer Homotopy. Ciprian Manolescu's talk on grid homology, combinatorial knot Floer homology, and Floer homotopy spectra.

https://kskedlaya.org/slides/columbia2021-handout.pdf, Banach Bundles and Nonarchimedean GAGA. Kiran Kedlaya's 32 slides on Banach vector bundles, the Fargues–Fontaine curve, and Banach–Colmez spaces.

https://server.mcm.ac.cn/~zheng/LI/Katz.pdf, Exponential Sums and Monodromy Groups. Nick Katz's lectures on $\ell$-adic local systems, geometric and arithmetic monodromy groups, and hypergeometric sheaves.

https://people.math.ethz.ch/~kowalski/applied-ell-adic.pdf, Applied l-Adic Cohomology. Emmanuel Kowalski's 79-page notes on trace functions, Kloosterman sums, and $\ell$-adic sheaves in analytic number theory.

https://people.math.ethz.ch/~kowalski/slides-conductors.pdf, Conductors and Complexity of Trace Functions. Kowalski's slides on bounding exponential sums, higher-dimensional trace functions, and Fourier complexity via Deligne's Weil II.

https://msp.cis.strath.ac.uk/types2025/slides/TYPES2025-slides8.pdf, Simplicial Type Theory. Formalization of Segal types, Rezk complete Segal types, and synthetic $\infty$-category theory.

https://webhomes.maths.ed.ac.uk/~v1ranick/slides/aber3.pdf, Algebraic K-Theory of Infinite Dihedral Groups. Andrew Ranicki's slides on the algebraic $K$-theory, class groups, and Whitehead torsion of infinite dihedral groups.

https://drive.google.com/file/d/1WnsN2SiUYGvDhTRunpEMf-HNn0vSrzjw/view, Constructions in Mirror Symmetry. Slide overview of the SYZ conjecture, mirror manifolds, and Picard–Fuchs equations.

https://www.math.ucla.edu/~pak/lectures/Slides/Moraga--Birational_maps_final--talk-slides--2024--short.pdf, Birational Geometry and Blow-Ups. Joaquín Moraga's slides on combinatorial projective spaces, blow-ups, flips, and the Minimal Model Program.

http://maine-quebec.mat.ulaval.ca/20/slides/Adam_Logan.pdf, Introduction to K3 Surfaces. Adam Logan's slides on the geometry, Hodge structures, and Picard lattices of $K3$ surfaces.

https://www.dpmms.cam.ac.uk/~or257/slides/Clay.pdf, Moduli Spaces and Homological Stability. Oscar Randal-Williams's slides on classifying spaces $B\operatorname{Diff}(M)$, mapping class groups $\Gamma_g$, and homological stability.

https://www.dpmms.cam.ac.uk/~or257/slides/BTM2013.pdf, Positive Scalar Curvature and Index Theory. Randal-Williams's slides on the index of Dirac operators and spaces of positive scalar curvature metrics.

https://www.dpmms.cam.ac.uk/~or257/slides/MFOPrize.pdf, Calculus of Manifold Embeddings. Survey on the Weiss embedding calculus, configuration spaces, and diffeomorphism groups.

https://www.dpmms.cam.ac.uk/~or257/slides/Skye.pdf, Metastability of Mapping Class Groups. Homological stability and high-dimensional metastabilities in mapping class groups.

https://www.dpmms.cam.ac.uk/~or257/slides/eCHT.pdf, Homological Stability in Topology. Comprehensive 35 slides on homological stability for symmetric groups, general linear groups, and moduli spaces of manifolds.

https://www.dpmms.cam.ac.uk/~or257/slides/coruna.pdf, Scanning Operations on Manifolds. Geometric scanning methods in the cohomology of moduli spaces of surfaces.

https://www.dpmms.cam.ac.uk/~or257/slides/Randal-Williams.pdf, Topology of Stable Moduli Spaces. Madsen–Weiss theorem, cobordism categories, and the Mumford conjecture on stable cohomology of $\mathcal{M}_g$.

https://www.dpmms.cam.ac.uk/~or257/slides/MIT2020.pdf, Smoothing Theory of Topological Manifolds. Obstructions to smooth structures on high-dimensional topological manifolds via Kirby–Siebenmann classes.

https://www.dpmms.cam.ac.uk/~or257/slides/Muenster2020.pdf, Diffeomorphisms of Disks I. Studies the homotopy types of diffeomorphism groups $\operatorname{Diff}(D^n, \partial D^n)$ and pseudoisotopies.

https://www.dpmms.cam.ac.uk/~or257/slides/Munster2024.pdf, Cohomology of Moduli Spaces. Case study in computing tautological rings and cohomology of moduli spaces of curves.

https://www.dpmms.cam.ac.uk/~or257/slides/Oxford2021.pdf, Tits Buildings and Diffeomorphism Groups. 68 slides on Tits buildings, Steinberg modules, and homological stability for arithmetic groups.

https://www.dpmms.cam.ac.uk/~or257/slides/Copenhagen2020.pdf, Diffeomorphism Groups and Cohomology. Advanced methods in studying classifying spaces of diffeomorphism groups.

https://www.dpmms.cam.ac.uk/~or257/slides/ICM2022.pdf, ICM Plenary on Diffeomorphisms of Manifolds. 72-page expanded survey on stable and unstable cohomology of moduli spaces of manifolds.

https://www.dpmms.cam.ac.uk/~or257/slides/Berlin2022.pdf, Homeomorphisms of Euclidean Space. 25 slides on $\operatorname{Homeo}(\mathbb{R}^d)$, topological groups, and orthogonal calculus.

https://www.dpmms.cam.ac.uk/~or257/slides/Delhi2021.pdf, Miller-Morita-Mumford Classes. Tautological rings of moduli spaces of Riemann surfaces and generalized MMM classes.

https://www.dpmms.cam.ac.uk/~or257/slides/Bonn.pdf, Steinberg Modules and Homological Stability. 65 slides on duality complexes, arithmetic groups, and stability.

https://www.dpmms.cam.ac.uk/~or257/slides/Banff2021.pdf, E-Infinity Algebras and General Linear Groups. $E_\infty$-ring spectra, algebraic $K$-theory, and the Nesterenko–Suslin theorem.

https://www.dpmms.cam.ac.uk/~or257/teaching/notes/Kthy_long.pdf, Characteristic Classes and K-Theory. 75-page lecture notes covering vector bundles, Chern classes, the splitting principle, and topological $K$-theory.

https://www.dpmms.cam.ac.uk/~or257/teaching/notes/at.pdf, Algebraic Topology Notes. 80-page notes on the fundamental group, covering spaces, singular homology, and the Mayer–Vietoris sequence.

https://math.stanford.edu/~vakil/22-245moduli/modulislides03-11.pdf, Moduli Spaces in Algebraic Geometry. Ravi Vakil's slides on representable functors, fine and coarse moduli spaces, and stack-theoretic criteria.

https://math.stanford.edu/~vakil/22-245moduli/, Course on Moduli Spaces. Homepage for Ravi Vakil's advanced algebraic geometry course on moduli spaces.

https://www.ub.edu/grega/JORGE/Slides/Martin.pdf, Principal Bundles with Formal Trivializations. Slides on moduli of $G$-bundles on curves and Serre's theorem on linearization.
