https://people.math.harvard.edu/~landesman/assets/torelli-theorem-notes.pdf, The Torelli Theorem for Curves. Detailed notes on reconstructing smooth projective curves from their polarized Jacobians $(\operatorname{Jac}(C), \Theta)$.

https://terrytao.wordpress.com/2008/11/05/concentration-compactness-and-the-profile-decomposition/, Concentration Compactness by Terence Tao. Lions' concentration-compactness principle and profile decompositions in non-compact PDE problems.

https://sebastiano.tronto.net/talks/slides-alggroupsfieldext.pdf, Algebraic Groups and Field Extensions. Sebastiano Tronto's 29 slides viewing algebraic groups $G/K$ as group-valued functors on field extensions $\mathbf{Field}_K \to \mathbf{Grp}$.

https://sebastiano.tronto.net/talks/slides-fieldextec.pdf, Field Extensions from Elliptic Curves. Division fields and Galois representations attached to torsion points on elliptic curves.

https://www.sas.rochester.edu/mth/sites/doug-ravenel/otherpapers/dwyer-CopenhagenSlides.pdf, Homotopy Theory of Homotopy Theories. 139 slides by Bill Dwyer on classifying models for $(\infty,1)$-categories: quasicategories, Segal categories, complete Segal spaces, and simplicial categories.

https://sebastiano.tronto.net/talks/notes-bilbao.pdf, Multiplication Endomorphisms on Abelian Varieties. Notes on the multiplication-by-$m$ map $[m]: A \to A$ and its kernel $A[m]$.

https://samsonzhou.github.io/CSCE658-S24/L2.pdf, Randomized Communication Complexity. Karger's min-cut algorithm via random edge contractions and the Schwartz–Zippel lemma.

https://samsonzhou.github.io/CSCE658-S24/L3.pdf, Concentration Inequalities in Algorithms. Using Markov and Chebyshev bounds to establish success guarantees for randomized algorithms.

https://samsonzhou.github.io/CSCE658-S24/L4.pdf, Moments and Tail Bounds. Calculating higher moments, variance bounds, and Chernoff bounds.

https://www.cl.cam.ac.uk/teaching/2122/RandAlgthm/lec1_intro_handout.pdf, Randomized Max-Cut Algorithms. Cambridge lecture notes on randomized approximation algorithms for Max-Cut.

https://www.cl.cam.ac.uk/teaching/2122/RandAlgthm/lec23_concentration_handout.pdf, Chernoff Bounds and Hoeffding's Lemma. Moment generating functions, sub-Gaussian variables, and exponential concentration bounds.

https://www.cl.cam.ac.uk/teaching/2122/RandAlgthm/lec4_markov_chains_handout.pdf, Markov Chains and Mixing. Stationary distributions, mixing times, and coupling methods.

https://lips.cs.princeton.edu/a-geometric-intuition-for-markovs-inequality/, Geometric Intuition for Markov's Inequality. Visualizing Markov's and Chebyshev's bounds via area under the probability distribution.

https://www.cl.cam.ac.uk/teaching/2122/RandAlgthm/lec5_randomwalk_hitting_handout.pdf, Random Walks on Graphs. Hitting times, commute times, and graph connectivity testing.

https://www.cl.cam.ac.uk/teaching/2122/RandAlgthm/lec67_linear_handout.pdf, Linear Programming Duality. Simplex algorithms, LP duality theorems, and min-cost flow relaxations.

https://www.cl.cam.ac.uk/teaching/2122/RandAlgthm/lec910_approximation_handout.pdf, Randomized Approximation Algorithms. Semidefinite programming (SDP) relaxations and the Goemans–Williamson Max-Cut algorithm.

https://www.cl.cam.ac.uk/teaching/2122/RandAlgthm/lec1112_spectral_handout.pdf, Spectral Graph Clustering. Graph Laplacians, Cheeger's inequality, and spectral partitioning algorithms.

https://www.cl.cam.ac.uk/teaching/2122/RandAlgthm/lec13_streaming_handout.pdf, Streaming Algorithms and Sketching. Alon–Matias–Szegedy (AMS) sketching, frequency moments $F_k$, and Morris's approximate counting.

https://www.cl.cam.ac.uk/teaching/2122/RandAlgthm/lec14_experts_handout.pdf, Multiplicative Weights Update. Online learning, regret minimization, and the multiplicative weights update algorithm.

https://www.cl.cam.ac.uk/teaching/2122/RandAlgthm/lec15_bandits_handout.pdf, Multi-Armed Bandit Algorithms. Exploration-exploitation trade-offs and Upper Confidence Bound (UCB) algorithms.

https://emilyriehl.github.io/files/context.pdf, Category Theory in Context. Emily Riehl's foundational textbook covering:
- **Preservation, Reflection, Creation:** Functors preserve limits (limit in domain $\implies$ limit in codomain), reflect limits (limit cone in image $\implies$ limit cone in domain), and create limits (limit in codomain $\implies$ unique limit lifting).
- **Kan Extensions:** Left Kan extensions $\operatorname{Lan}_K F$ (generalizing colimits) and right Kan extensions $\operatorname{Ran}_K F$ (generalizing limits). Pointwise Kan extensions are computed via limit formulas over comma categories: $\operatorname{Ran}_K F(d) \cong \lim (d/K \to \mathcal{C} \to \mathcal{E})$.
- **Yoneda & Density:** The Yoneda lemma as a pointwise Kan extension. The co-Yoneda lemma expresses every presheaf as a colimit of representables: $F(c) \cong \operatorname{colim}(\mathcal{C}/c \to \mathcal{C} \to \mathbf{Set})$.
- **Codensity Monads:** The right Kan extension of a functor along itself. The codensity monad of $\mathbf{FinSet} \hookrightarrow \mathbf{Set}$ is the ultrafilter monad.

https://pages.jh.edu/rrynasi1/FoundationsOFMath/Literature/Toposes/Johnstone1977ToposTheory.pdf, Johnstone's Topos Theory. Foundational 1977 monograph on elementary topoi, subobject classifiers, and the Yoneda density theorem.

https://intlpress.com/site/pub/files/_fulltext/journals/hha/2016/0018/0001/HHA-2016-0018-0001-a003.pdf, Dwyer-Kan Simplicial Localization. Simplicial localization $L^W \mathcal{C}$ formally inverting weak equivalences while preserving all higher homotopy coherences.

https://sebastiano.tronto.net/talks/notes-etale.pdf, Introduction to Etale Cohomology. Notes explaining why Zariski cohomology of constant sheaves vanishes on irreducible varieties and how étale sites $\operatorname{Et}(X)$ resolve this.

https://sebastiano.tronto.net/talks/kummer-tarrach.pdf, Kummer Theory for Elliptic Curves. 15 slides on chord-and-tangent group laws and Kummer extensions generated by division points.

https://sebastiano.tronto.net/talks/division-groningen.pdf, Division Fields and Ideal Filters. Seminar slides on ideal filters and division fields of abelian varieties.

https://sebastiano.tronto.net/talks/slides-journey-kummer.pdf, Kummer Extensions and Galois Representations. Cyclotomic Kummer extensions $K(\sqrt[n]{a})$ and explicit Galois degrees.

https://sebastiano.tronto.net/talks/slides-kummer-final.pdf, Kummer Theory for Algebraic Groups. Generalizing Kummer theory to commutative algebraic groups and division points $K(n^{-1}A)$.

https://sebastiano.tronto.net/talks/slides-groupcohomec.pdf, Group Cohomology and Elliptic Curves. 22 slides covering 1-cocycles $\phi(gh) = \phi(g) + g\phi(h)$, 1-coboundaries $\phi(g) = gm - m$, and the classification of the Selmer and Shafarevich–Tate groups $\operatorname{Sha}(E/K)$.

https://www.math.uchicago.edu/~eskin/luminy2012/lectures.pdf, SL(2, R)-Actions on Moduli Spaces. Alex Eskin's lectures on Teichmüller dynamics, $\mathrm{SL}_2(\mathbb{R})$-actions on moduli spaces of abelian differentials, and Ratner-like theorems.

https://warwick.ac.uk/fac/sci/maths/people/staff/sheth/sl2.pdf, Representations of sl(2, C). 45 slides providing a self-contained introduction to $\mathfrak{sl}_2(\mathbb{C})$: Lie brackets $[x, y] = xy - yx$, adjoint representations, Casimir operators, and highest-weight classifications.

https://math.gmu.edu/~rallen2/slides/gmu/lie%20algebra.pdf, Lie Algebras and Lie Cohomology. 81 slides covering simple/semisimple Lie algebras, Killing forms, universal enveloping algebras, and Chevalley–Eilenberg cohomology $H^*(\mathfrak{g}, M)$.

https://hal.science/hal-02498233v1/file/Derived_deformation_algebraic_structures_10_2019.pdf, Derived Deformation Theory. Survey on formal moduli problems, differential graded Lie algebras, and $L_\infty$-algebras.

https://sanathdevalapurkar.github.io/files/rh-slides.pdf, The Riemann-Hilbert Correspondence. 61 slides on the equivalence between regular holonomic $\mathcal{D}$-modules and constructible perverse sheaves.

https://pcwww.liv.ac.uk/~jonwoolf/Slides/OberwolfachSlides.pdf, Intersection Cohomology and Stratified Spaces. Jon Woolf's slides on Whitney stratifications, perverse sheaves, and intersection spaces.

https://people.maths.ox.ac.uk/joyce/DublinTalk2.pdf, Wall-Crossing Formulas via Vertex Algebras. Dominic Joyce on Joyce structures, wall-crossing formulas for Donaldson–Thomas invariants, and vertex algebras.

https://www.math.stonybrook.edu/~bishop/classes/math638.F20/wp_notes.pdf, Weil-Petersson Metric and Curves. Christopher Bishop's 252 slides on the Weil–Petersson metric on universal Teichmüller space, quasiconformal mappings, and O'Hara knot energies.

https://www.math.stonybrook.edu/~bishop/classes/math670.F23/notes1.pdf, Riemann Surfaces and Dessins d'Enfants. Notes on branched coverings, Belyi's theorem, and Grothendieck's dessins d'enfants.

https://www.math.stonybrook.edu/~bishop/lectures/Barcelona1.pdf, Finite Trees and Riemann Surfaces. Lectures on conformal welding, true trees, and polynomial dynamics.

https://www.math.stonybrook.edu/~bishop/lectures/Barcelona2.pdf, Complex Dynamics and Shabat Polynomials. Connecting planar trees to Shabat polynomials and Belyi maps.

https://people.maths.ox.ac.uk/joyce/DAG2022/, Dominic Joyce's Course on Derived Algebraic Geometry. Comprehensive lecture series and slides covering derived schemes, stacks, Kuranishi spaces, and virtual invariants.

https://people.maths.ox.ac.uk/joyce/AberdeenTalk.pdf, What is Derived Geometry? Expository lecture explaining why derived geometry is essential for non-transverse intersections and counting problems.

https://people.maths.ox.ac.uk/joyce/MMThandout1.pdf, Donaldson-Thomas Theory I. Notes on moduli of stable coherent sheaves on Calabi–Yau 3-folds and DT invariants.

https://people.maths.ox.ac.uk/joyce/MMThandout2.pdf, Donaldson-Thomas Theory II. Shifted symplectic structures and Behrend functions $\nu_M: M \to \mathbb{Z}$.

https://people.maths.ox.ac.uk/joyce/MMThandout3.pdf, Donaldson-Thomas Theory III. Categorification of DT invariants and wall-crossing formulas.

https://people.maths.ox.ac.uk/joyce/ToulouseHandout1.pdf, Derived Differential Geometry I. Dominic Joyce on $C^\infty$-rings, $C^\infty$-schemes, and derived manifolds (d-manifolds).

https://people.maths.ox.ac.uk/joyce/ToulouseHandout2.pdf, Derived Differential Geometry II. Square-zero extensions, $m$-Kuranishi neighborhoods, and virtual dimensions.

https://people.maths.ox.ac.uk/joyce/ToulouseHandout3.pdf, Derived Differential Geometry III. Virtual fundamental classes and bordism formulations of derived counting invariants.

https://people.maths.ox.ac.uk/joyce/DAG2022/DAG1+2.pdf, Derived Algebraic Geometry Slides 1-2. Moduli problems, why fine moduli schemes fail, Artin/Deligne–Mumford stacks, and derived intersections without transversality.

https://people.maths.ox.ac.uk/joyce/DAG2022/DAG3+4.pdf, Derived Algebraic Geometry Slides 3-4. 2-categories of stacks, quotient stacks $[U/G]$, classifying stacks $[*/G] \cong BG$, and smooth atlases.

https://people.maths.ox.ac.uk/joyce/DAG2022/DAG5+6.pdf, Derived Algebraic Geometry Slides 5-6. Coarse vs fine moduli spaces, Gieseker stability, compactifications of moduli of vector bundles via torsion-free sheaves, and skyscraper sheaves.

https://people.maths.ox.ac.uk/joyce/DAG2022/DAG7+8.pdf, Derived Algebraic Geometry Slides 7-8. Triangulated categories, bounded derived categories $\mathcal{D}^b(\operatorname{Coh} X)$, and quasi-isomorphisms.

https://ncatlab.org/nlab/show/Kan+extension, Definition of Kan Extensions. Rapid reference defining left and right Kan extensions $\operatorname{Lan}_K F$ and $\operatorname{Ran}_K F$ via universal arrows and comma categories.

https://people.maths.ox.ac.uk/joyce/DAG2022/DAG9+10.pdf, Derived Algebraic Geometry Slides 9-10. Simplicial $K$-algebras, model categories, and derived commutative rings.

https://people.maths.ox.ac.uk/joyce/DAG2022/DAG11+12.pdf, Derived Algebraic Geometry Slides 11-12. Cotangent complexes $\mathbb{L}_X$ and obstruction theory in derived geometry.

https://people.maths.ox.ac.uk/joyce/DAG2022/DAG13+14.pdf, Derived Algebraic Geometry Slides 13-14. Virtual fundamental classes $[M]^{\text{vir}}$, Behrend-Fantechi obstruction theories, and Donaldson–Thomas invariants on Calabi–Yau 3-folds.

https://people.maths.ox.ac.uk/joyce/VertexAlgebras2021/VA1+2.pdf, Vertex Algebras Course 1-2. Dominic Joyce on the formal definition of vertex algebras, state-field correspondences $Y(v, z)$, and Borcherds identity.

https://people.maths.ox.ac.uk/joyce/VertexAlgebras2021/VA3+4.pdf, Vertex Algebras Course 3-4. Conformal vertex algebras, Virasoro actions, and vertex operator modules.

https://people.maths.ox.ac.uk/joyce/VertexAlgebras2021/VA5+6.pdf, Vertex Algebras Course 5-6. Lattice vertex algebras and the Monster vertex algebra $V^\natural$.

https://people.maths.ox.ac.uk/joyce/VertexAlgebras2021/VA7+8.pdf, Vertex Algebras Course 7-8. Wall-crossing structures and vertex Lie algebras in Donaldson–Thomas
